ParlaMint-PL_2019-01-30-sejm-77-1.u14	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! The draft Parental Supplementary Witness Act, which I am pleased to present today to the High House, is an expression of the announcement made by Prime Minister Mateusz Morawiecki in April Ub. on the creation of a system of benefits for mothers who gave birth and raised at least four children and did not earn a pension financed by the social security system. It is a project that solves a serious social problem, which is the existence of a significant, because of the number of women, mothers who have met the condition of birth and upbringing at least four children and do not have the right to a pension financed by the social security system of at least the lowest amount of pension. This is a highly unsatisfactory situation not to say scandalous. The proposed solution should be looked at from a systemic point of view, i.e. from the point of view of today's social security system, solutions which are pro-family in nature, which encourage the decision to enlarge the family. Such a system, which has been in force since 1 January 1999, is the obligation to finance contributions to pension and pension insurance for persons who benefit from parental leave from the State budget. In September 2013, the Law amending the Social Insurance Act, which extended the subject-matter of this right to finance pension benefits - from those taking parental leave, entered into force... In a similar situation, from 1 January 1999 there have been beneficiaries of maternity allowance. As I mentioned, since September 2013, this obligation to finance contributions from the state budget has been extended to other categories of people who, when they devote themselves to their child-rearing activities, are either resigning from their job or not taking it. Despite these solutions already in force in the social security system today, we have 85 thousand. mothers who have had at least four children and are deprived of the right to a pension of at least the lowest pension. According to our estimates, 85,000. It consists, first, of a group of people whose number is estimated at around 65 thousand who do not have the right to a pension at all, and, secondly, of a group whose number is estimated at around 21 000 who, although they have the right to a pension, but who are entitled to this benefit, are less than the lowest pension benefit. This benefit, which we are proposing in the parent supplemental provision bill discussed today, is to sort of eliminate, eliminate this flaw, this gap that exists today in the pension system. It is also worth taking a closer look at these two categories of people whom I have just mentioned, who will be entitled to parental supplementary benefits. If we are talking about this group of about 65 000 people who do not have the right to a pension, this group is largely composed of those covered by the old pension scheme, i.e. persons born before 1 January 1949. I draw attention to the fact that in the old pension system, in the so-called. a defined benefit scheme, a period of insurance, a premium and a non-contributory period of at least 20 years was a condition for the right to a pension. For this number, 65 thousand. it consists of persons who have not fulfilled this condition. Internship is for women of 20 years, for men of 25 years. These are persons who did not meet the condition of achieving the required period of insurance. When it comes to a group of people - it is a smaller group, i.e. About 21,000. persons - who are entitled to a pension, but less than the lowest pension, here it can be said that they are persons who are covered by the new pension scheme, i.e. were born after 31 December 1948, but also did not meet these internship requirements. I would point out that, in the new pension scheme, achieving a premium and non-contributory period is not a condition for the right to a pension. For the second group of persons, the benefit to be granted to them will be the difference between the amount of the lowest pension benefit - from 1 March 2019 it will be PLN 1100 - and the amount of pension and pension benefits collected by those persons. The draft parent benefit law sets out the conditions and modalities for granting this benefit to persons who have born and raised or raised at least four children, also lays down the criteria for granting the right to that benefit and the rules for paying and financing it. The purpose of the parental supplementary benefit will be to provide the means of living of the above mentioned. persons and the fight against the poverty of this group. The acquisition of the right to benefit will not be linked to the earlier payment of social security contributions in Poland. The parent supplement will be able to grant, at the request of the person concerned, either the President of the Social Insurance Institution or the President of the Agricultural Social Insurance Fund, by administrative decision, after examining whether the person concerned fulfils all the criteria laid down in the proposed regulation. A parent's supplementary benefit may be granted to a mother who has given birth and raised at least four children, and in the event of the death of the mother of the child, the abandonment of children by the mother, as well as in the event of a prolonged cessation of personal care for the child, including the father who raised at least four children. As regards the criteria for granting parental benefit, it will be essential to reach the age of 60 for mothers, 65 for fathers. It will also be a condition not to have the necessary means of subsistence. One of the conditions will be the possession of Polish citizenship or the right of permanent residence in the territory of the Republic of Poland in the case of nationals of the Member States of the European Union, EFTA States - parties to the Agreement on the European Economic Area or the Swiss Confederation, as well as being a foreigner legally residing in the territory of the Republic of Poland. The benefit will be granted to persons residing in the territory of the Republic of Poland and having, after the age of 16, a centre of personal or economic interest in the territory of our country for a period of at least 10 years. Where the person concerned is already receiving a benefit lower than the amount of the lowest pension, the parental benefit will supplement the benefit collected up to that amount. In the case of persons who are not entitled to any benefit, the amount of this benefit will correspond to the amount of the lowest pension. The bill will enter into force on 1 March 2019. Finally, I would like to draw attention to another aspect of the proposed regulation. Namely, I would like to draw the attention of the High House to the fact that if we look at the general pension system in Poland today, we will see that the characteristic of this system is its repartition, i.e. financing expenditure of the Social Insurance Fund in the case of persons who have already reached retirement age, from the current income of that fund derived from the contributions of persons to the so-called. working age, paid by persons in so-called. the working age. We are talking about funding or the intergenerational nature of the pension system. As far as the substance of the proposed regulation is concerned, it is intended to honor mothers who have born and raised or raised at least four children and have therefore resigned from employment or have not taken up employment. We can say that we are honoring the contribution of mothers who have contributed to increasing the number of generations of people who today, as part of this generation of children of post-production age, finance social security contributions. We can say that we are solving a problem that has not so far been solved within the framework of the general pension insurance system, but also within the framework of the farmers' social security system. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

On the other hand, I would like to say to this law that it is possible - and this must be clearly said - thanks to the inclusion of educational leave introduced in 2012. Then we secured in the state budget about PLN 1400 million for the contribution of pension leave to both those who work and those who have been uninsured so far. This law contains a certain interesting and important idea, while execution itself is no longer good. Firstly, the Act grants pensions to people who have not worked, has not earned a pension, has given birth and has raised at least four children. Why are many children's mothers who have shared child care with work not appreciated? Very often, because of the family situation, they usually did low-paid work and their pensions are minimal. According to the government, should a woman who's never worked and a woman with 40 years' work experience get the same money? Secondly, the project raises even more controversy with regard to the situation of women on the so-called. EWK pensions, namely mothers of disabled people who, after working for 20 years, went to early retirement to take care of sick children. Their benefits today amount to PLN 700.800, half the amount of the care provision, less than the lowest pension. There are about 33 thousand such women in Poland. These people live with disabled adults at the limit of minimum existence and feel punished for working. It's inhumane to leave them to themselves when more money is given to people who have never worked. Therefore, we are calling for the so-called increase as soon as possible. EWK pensions up to the amount of the care provision, preferably from the date of application of the law in question. Thirdly, this law requires clarification on at least two points. The first point concerns Article. 2 which specifies that the designer understands the child's own child or spouse or the adopted child. Consider this case when the mother of two children is involved with the father of another two, three children, and after a month of marriage she is applying for a pension for four children. We propose that the rules be clarified by defining certain time-limits. The second point that we think needs to be clarified is Article. 5, which says that it is possible to refuse to grant the benefit to a person who has been deprived of parental authority or whose court has limited parental authority by placing the child in foster care. Should a pension for raising a child be allowed in situations where parental authority is limited without directing to a foster family? Let's think about it. In conclusion, we will present amendments during the second reading and, of course, we will take an active part in the work of the committee. The law needs to be clarified, but a system of incentives should also be created and work ethos should be rewarded, because the condition for a rich society is to build a work ethos and it is worth keeping this in mind, and then - after hearing earlier speeches - this law will be recognised by the whole House. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-01-30-sejm-77-1.u130	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Thank you very much for, I would say, a rich or extensive discussion, for the various threads that you have put forward in this discussion. I will try to respond to the comments that have been made, probably not to everyone, but I think that many answers to many of the questions I have asked will result from perhaps a more detailed explanation by me of what I started with today, i.e. from an attempt to put this draft law in place under today's existing arrangements for the general pension system. I have pointed out to you that we are talking about a new pension system, i.e. the pension scheme, which we call the system of defined contributions - the pension scheme, which can be said to depend directly on the amount of the pension benefit on the amount of the contributions paid during the period covered by the insurance, as well as on the termination of the professional activity. This system of defined contributions includes legal solutions, financial solutions that reward parenting, which encourage decision-making to enlarge the family. I drew attention to this, and you also mentioned in the discussion that since the start of the new pension system, we have in this system taken over by the budget of the State the burden of financing contributions to pension and pension insurance for people receiving maternity benefits, i.e. those taking maternity leave and parental leave, as well as paternity leave. Let me remind you that this is now a period of the year, a period of 12 months. In addition, the state budget has taken on the burden of financing contributions to pension insurance for persons on parental leave. Let me remind you that this is a total period of 3 years. If we add these two periods together, we will get a 4-year period during which the state budget finances contributions to the pension insurance of a person who resigns from his or her job or suspends his or her job and devotes himself to raising a child in the first period of his or her life. I have also drawn your attention to the fact that since 2013 this range of funding has been significantly broader, because it includes not only persons who, due to their employment relationship, have been able to take parental, maternity, paternity, parental and educational leave, but also persons who are subject to pension insurance from other titles, i.e. for non-agricultural activities, for work under civil law contracts, for cooperation and even for the service of the clergy. I would also like to draw your attention to the fact that since September 2013, the state budget has financed contributions to the pension insurance of persons who have not had the period before which they have taken care of the child or started to raise the child, that period. It can be said that these are the family policies that are present in the pension insurance system. You're asking me if we don't remember our fathers. So I would like to draw your attention to the fact that both working mothers and fathers, in accordance with the Labour Code, who are undertaking child rearing, can already benefit from these powers that I have just mentioned. You could say that the decision... [[That's not true.]] Except for the 14-month period of maternity leave... [[Voice from the room: Weekly.]] ...14 weeks, I apologize when it is actually a maternity leave dedicated to a woman, but in order to regenerate after birth or also allow child care in the first period of the child's life. After 14 weeks after birth, the real decision whether the mother or father will benefit from these solutions is simply made in agreement between the parents. [[No, you're not, Minister, you're not.]] You are also asking me whether today's pension system provides for some differentiation of parental support depending on the number of children who remain in the family. Ladies and gentlemen! It's a simple mathematical mechanism. If we are talking about a four-year period for the financing of pension contributions per child, the higher the number of children and the number of children born at appropriate intervals, the longer the state budget finances the pension contributions. In such an extreme case, so we can say, in the case of four children, if children were born in four-year cycles, there is a sixteen-year period for the financing of contributions to pension insurance or to the mother or the father who takes care of the children. So these are the solutions that are already in the pension system today. And, now, if, from this perspective, we look at the solutions that we propose in the draft parental supplementary benefit law, then, as I mentioned in my speech earlier, we see that we are talking about solving some of the problems, some of the shortcomings that occurred in the old pension system, the shortcomings of this - the state also in this discussion you pointed out - that the period of child care was not treated as a contribution period in the old pension system. And that's what honoring the difficulty of raising children, taking care of children in accordance with this law is all about. This law does not change the assumptions of the general pension system, the assumptions which are based on contributions, because the burden of funding parent supplementary benefits is borne by the state budget. The analysis of the assumptions of this bill leads to the conclusion that this is not an insurance benefit, it is a supply benefit, because the financing is not from the Social Insurance Fund but from the State budget. ParlaMint-PL_2019-01-30-sejm-77-1.u134	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Seym! On behalf of the group of Members, I have the honour to present a draft law amending the electoral code law, print no. 3163. I would like to remind the High House that in January 2018 the Sejm passed a law to increase citizens' participation in the process of selecting, operating and controlling certain public authorities. The Act introduced numerous changes in the area of electoral law, in particular those relating to elections to local government units. The proposed draft amending the electoral code synchronizes the changes introduced in January 2018 with those relating to the other elections, which in turn is intended to ensure a more efficient electoral procedure in the case of other elections. I would like to stress that the proposed changes are the changes signalled and expected by the State Election Commission and the National Election Office. I would like to point out that the electoral code itself is an extremely extensive law, contains 517 articles, and the proposed proposal contains changes that cover only 32 articles. Allow me to discuss the proposed changes in chronological terms and, of course, I will discuss the most important ones, apart from purely technical changes. Ladies and gentlemen! Amendment 1 refers to Article. 5 The electoral code and is closely linked to the 19th amendment, which refers to the article. 181 electoral code. I would like to stress that the regional electoral commission for voting in the circuit boards and the regional electoral commission for determining the results of the vote is set up only in national local elections. That means we leave only one committee in the remaining elections. Local elections are so complicated and require such extensive work that it is justified to set up two committees: one to vote and the other to determine the results. The next amendment refers to Article. 11 in Chapter 2, which regulates electoral rights. In this article, we propose a new provision in order to align the provisions of the electoral code with the provisions of the law on local workers by regulating in the electoral code the issue of passive electoral rights in a way similar to that of regulating the rules on local workers employed on the basis of elections or appointments. According to the change, in the election, the governor of the right of choice will not have a person sentenced to any penalty by a final judgment of the court for a mental offence prosecuted by a public charge or a deliberate tax offence, and not, as yet, only for a sentence of imprisonment. The adoption of this amendment will eliminate the existing legal conflict between the two laws. This is also a request from the State Election Commission. The next amendment refers to Article. 20. I would like to signal that, in the current legal state, the decision whether or not to include a person in the electoral register shall be made by the governor, the mayor, the president of the city within 3 days of the date of the application. The project envisages extending the deadline for examining the application and issuing a decision on the right of entry or refusal to enter in the electoral register for up to 5 days. This is a proposal from local government. The mayors, mayors and presidents indicated that verification over a period of three days is too short. Ladies and gentlemen! It is also worth mentioning the change we propose in the article. 35, which makes it clear that a non-national citizen of the European Union may vote on the basis of a certificate of the right to vote issued by the consulate. Another important change - appears - is contained in Article. 153, where an additional paragraph is introduced, which states that the electoral commissioner has the right to act as chairman of a district or district electoral commission or to act as a member. Here we remove the collision between the three articles of the electoral code. Another major change appears in Article. 161. Records introduce the possibility for the State Election Commission to adopt resolutions on matters of urgency on a regular basis when it is not possible to convene a committee meeting. It is worth noting that some tasks are subject to a short deadline, e.g. It is not enough to adopt such a resolution, therefore the draft law introduces such a solution and meets the expectations of the State Election Commission and improves the electoral process. Another major change appears in Article. 170. In the current state of law, Commissioners need not be judges. It is proposed to amend the rules in such a way that the District Election Commissions consist of four to 10 judges and, as its chairman, the electoral commissioner, and four judges and of their own, as its chairman, the electoral commissioner. I would like to stress that the Commissioner for Elections does not need to be a judge at the moment, and here we are also removing a certain inaccuracy. It is worth mentioning the changes to Article. 182. In this change we want to diversify the compositions of electoral committees and these warehouses will be conditioned by the number of residents in the circuit. Other provisions of Article 182 describe in detail, ladies and gentlemen, the mechanism for the appointment of members to the regional electoral commission from among the persons notified by the electoral committees and, if the committees fail to exercise their rights, the application of candidates shall be left to the electoral commissioner, who shall complement the committee to the full membership of the voters meeting the condition in paragraph 4, even if the 18th year of age has been completed. It is also important to change the deadline for the submission of candidates for membership, which is harmonised up to 30 days before the election day. Another change in this article is that the candidate for a member of the regional electoral commission must be 18 years old at the date of notification to the committee, not the day of the election, as has been the case so far. This seems very reasonable because such a person then performs a lot of legal work and is not really an adult. Amendment relating to Article. 184 regulates the matter of kinship. It seems that a person related to a candidate in the election may be a member of a regional electoral commission appropriate to other electoral districts than a district in which a person related to him or her is a candidate. It seems that to this day this record has been too restrictive. At the moment, we clarify so that such a person can sit and work on other committees outside the committee from which a related person is running. This Article shall also be supplemented by the addition of the power to dismiss a member of the committee to the electoral commissioner in certain situations. We point out these cases: not participating in the work without justification or taking illegal action. The Commissioner has a plenipotence to call off such a person. Ladies and gentlemen! It is worth paying attention to the next change that appears in the article. 218, which fulfils the order of the Constitutional Court, or, in fact, the judgment of the Constitutional Court of 18 July 2012, which found that part of the provision of the Article. Point (b) is replaced by the following: 2 concerning the absence of an appeal in this respect is incompatible with the Constitution. This provision has therefore become obsolete, but has so far remained unchanged in the electoral code. Therefore, we change that the State Elections Commission's decision shall be entitled to an action before the Supreme Court within 2 days of the date of publication by the CIC and the Supreme Court shall examine the action within 2 days of the formation of three judges in non-trial proceedings. Ladies and gentlemen! There are changes in the appeal procedure and they relate both to Article. 300, Art. 304, Art. 404 or Art. 405. They regulate this appeal mode and - you can say - there is a wording here that is the key: that this mode runs from the date of publication of the order to the public by the PKW. The State Election Commission has pointed out that very often electoral authorities have had difficulty reaching electoral representatives and phone calls have not been received. I would like to stress that the State Election Commission is not a detective institution or the Polish Red Cross, to search for people, is only to do their work in this very intense time. That is why such a change occurs in these four articles. To conclude my speech, I would like to stress that according to the proposal, the law is to enter into force on the day following the date of the announcement due to the important interests of the State, and the principles of the democratic rule of law do not prevent this law from being adopted. The project has a positive socio-economic impact, even by the fact that there is one committee, not as yet in the legislation - two in all elections. The subject matter of the proposed regulation is not covered by European Union law. So much for me. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-01-30-sejm-77-1.u186	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! [[Game in the O.R., bell ringing.]] High Izbo! I hereby submit a report to the Committee on Digitisation, Innovation and Modern Technologies on the Government's draft law amending the telecoms law and certain other laws, print No 3156. First of all, I would like to briefly signal the importance and importance of this project. The need to amend the telecoms law is directly linked to the infringement procedure initiated by the European Commission in 2016 concerning the incorrect application of Directive 2002/20/EC on the authorisation of electronic communications networks and services, as well as the incorrect application of Directive 2002/21/EC on the common regulatory framework for electronic communications networks and services - namely the Framework Directive - and Directive 2002/77/EC on competition in the markets for electronic communications networks and services, as well as the incorrect transposition of the Directive on the authorisation of electronic communications networks and services. The European Commission has pointed out that there is no correct transposition of Article. Article 14 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the Authorisation Directive as regards the obligation to consult planned changes to frequency bookings and raised reservations on the rules governing the conduct of auctions and tenders. The doubts relate in particular to the non-binding nature of the tenders submitted during the auction, the lack of financial consequences of withdrawal from the reservation or the insufficient amount of the security. Imperfections in the auction procedure identified by the European Commission were directly translated into the course of Poland's only 800 MHz auction in 2015. The main objective of the amendment is therefore to improve the rules governing selective treatment for frequency booking and thus avoid imposing very high penalties on Poland for continuing to maintain the incorrect transposition into Polish law. Of course, the project also introduces some important changes, so that the rare good, such as radio frequencies, is distributed in Poland and used as efficiently as possible. Among the extremely necessary tools and mechanisms introduced by the project, enabling efficient frequency management in Poland, it should be possible, for example, to define the timing of the distribution of individual frequencies, to conduct selection procedures for busy frequencies, so that the new entity can start using them immediately on the date of their release, or to enable the President of UKE to convert the use of frequencies to other frequencies with similar characteristics. Therefore, the comprehensive proposals envisaged in the project will help to organise spectrum in a given band and will allow for example. to make the necessary changes to change the use of the 700 MHz band for wireless services or the allocation of frequencies for the 5G network. In addition, the draft contains regulations adapting national provisions to those resulting from the Union regulatory directives, namely Directive 2014/53EU of the European Parliament and of the Council on the harmonisation of the laws of the Member States relating to the making available on the market of radio equipment and repealing Directive 1999/5EC. High Izbo! The Commission for Digitisation, Innovation and Modern Technologies, to which this project was addressed for consideration, addressed it at its meeting on 17 January 2019. The Commission has carefully examined the draft law by adopting several technical and legislative amendments tabled by the Legislative Office. The Commission also accepted one corrective amendment, tabled by the Legislative Office, concerning a transitional provision concerning the time limit for examining the application for inclusion of a device in the register of radio equipment used without authorisation. High Izbo! Given the key importance of the project for the further development of the telecommunications market and new technologies, the committee recommends that the report be adopted in accordance with the submission. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-01-30-sejm-77-1.u188	Dear Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present the position of the club as regards the government draft law amending the telecoms law and certain other laws, print No 3116. This is an extremely important bill because developing telecommunications is one of the basic pillars of a well-growing economy. The amendment of the Law of 16 July 2004 concerns provisions on frequency management, radio equipment and frequency charges. The new rules adapt the Polish right to regulation of the European Union. The changes are also linked to the need to increase the efficiency of radio spectrum management and, in particular, to ensure an increased interest in the deployment of new technologies on the telecommunications market. One of the most important changes proposed is the provisions that allow the 700 MHz band to be re-routed or the frequency reservation procedure to be improved. The revision allows the Minister of Digitalisation to define a timetable for the allocation of certain spectrum resources so that State policy on spectrum management can be implemented. At the same time, the amendment proposed to modify the rules for the award of frequency reservations for the next period, including in particular the introduction of an additional condition for the President of the Office for Electronic Communications to refuse such an extension, and to allow the President of UKE to announce on its own initiative a tender, auction or competition for the reservation of frequencies not only if he has knowledge of the interest in frequency resources that are already available. It also enabled the UKE President, by decision, to change the allocated frequency bookings in order to ensure the governance of the frequency management and to increase the efficiency of the frequencies used. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The amendment of the bill goes in a good, expected direction. The proposed solutions are intended to promote socio-economic development, which is essential for legislative changes. The bill presented responds to the expectations of the social side, hence many positive opinions concerning this matter. On behalf of the Law and Justice Club, I ask the High House to adopt this bill while at the same time submitting one amendment on behalf of my club. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The second amendment also responds to the proposals presented in the public consultation. Namely, it is now assumed that the date on which the application for renewal is to be submitted is no earlier than 3 years and no later than 2 years after the end of the reservation. We propose that these provisions be amended in order to provide for appropriate time limits for this application to be submitted no earlier than 4 years before the end of the period of validity of the right to have these frequencies available and no later than one year before the expiry date. So that's the second point, the second amendment. The third amendment complements the existing provision on the registration of equipment by indicating that not only the owner of the reservation can request entry in the register but also the entity authorised by it. And that would be appropriate and similar to the issues that are related to other radio devices. This is the third amendment. And the fourth amendment is also very... Maybe I'll put it another way. A major discussion during the committee meeting also triggered a provision of a law which assumes that in an area of 0.5 km in diameter around the measuring devices of the Office of Electronic Communications no other radio equipment can be set up and that it will not be possible to issue permissions to set such devices in this area, which may also indicate that in the age of the challenge of the 5G Internet, which we are facing, in large cities where there are measuring devices, the so-called. white spots. Therefore, it should be noted that under the current compaction of radio equipment, monitoring tasks or checking and measuring emission sources can be effectively carried out using mobile measuring stations, and we therefore believe that, [[♪ The bell ♪]] that this provision should not be concluded. Therefore, and with the opinion that I have presented, on behalf of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club - the Civic Coalition I am placing four amendments to this bill on your hands. Thank you. ParlaMint-PL_2019-01-30-sejm-77-1.u214	Dear Members, Dear Messengers, Dear Members, First of all, I would like to start with the theme of the timetable, which has already been raised several times and in committee, and during today's sitting. I have referred on several occasions to the allegations you have made, I have referred to them in substance, and I have the impression that I have not once heard my answers from you and taken into account, which is a clear signal to me that your comments are not of a substantive nature, they are merely political Tromtadration, and that is why they are being raised all the time. [[Applause]] But... [[Voice from the room: Bravo!]] ...I'll resume the schedule. The provisions on the timetable that we have introduced, which we proposed in the revision of the telecoms law, are primarily intended to close down the system that operates in Poland today. This country - Poland as a Member State, the Government of the Republic of Poland - is responsible to the European Union for fulfilling the obligations of the Polish State regarding the deadlines for distribution and allocation of bands for specific activities. On the other hand, the government does not have any tools that would allow it to comply with these obligations. The President of the Office for Electronic Communications has these tools. The proposed timetable is intended only to close this system and to ensure that the government can effectively enforce compliance with EU commitments from the regulator. I wanted to emphasise here the fact that this is not a breach. Today in the article. Article 189 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 1 Telecommunications law is explicitly stated that both the President of UKE and the Government are regulatory bodies. Moreover, the proposed amendment is part of the current legal order, because it is already in the article today. The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation in accordance with Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council concerning the European Parliament and of the Council concerning the implementation of Council Regulation (EC, Euratom) No 1605/2002 on the financial rules applicable to the general budget of the Union and repealing Council Regulation (EC, Euratom) No 1605/2002 on the Financial Regulation applicable to the general budget of the European Communities and repealing Council Regulation (EC, Euratom) No 1605/2002 on the Financial Regulation applicable to the general budget of the European Communities (OJ L 298, 26.10.2012, p. 1). 3 It is pointed out that the National Board of Frequencies is defined by the Council of Ministers, and that the President of the Office of Electronic Communications is an entity which is obliged to carry out what is specified in the National Board of Frequency Purposes, these obligations. The introduction of the schedule shall in no way affect the competence of the telecoms regulator in the distribution of frequency bands and its exclusive competence in this area. Only the President of UKE shall continue to be the authority empowered to prepare proceedings in this field, to conduct selection, assignment, withdrawal and frequency booking procedures. In addition, the draft amendment extends its competences e.g. the possibility of announcing selection proceedings on resources which will only be available in the future. I stress: the timetable does not agree with the principle of the regulator's independence, it is not an instructional one, and the Minister is limited in law when setting it - he issues a timetable in the form of a regulation, the regulation is consulted, it must comply with the radio-communication rules, with the policy and coordination of radio spectrum set out in the multiannual Union programmes. Therefore, it cannot be alleged that they agree with the independence of the regulator. We reviewed these proposals, consulted them with the Ministry of Foreign Affairs and consulted them on a work basis with the European Commission. There's no doubt about that here. And I've already told you at least three times. Furthermore, I would like to stress that the proposed timetable takes into account your needs for implementation, the development of 5G technology in Poland. It is this schedule in comparison with the amended law today, with the so-called. With a mega-law, a law to support the development of services and telecommunications networks, it will provide tools for this technology to exist in Poland. The timetable will set a deadline for the distribution of bands, which will be a clear signal for the telecommunications market, which today does not exist, because, as you have noticed, there is a strategy, and if you open the text of the strategy, the strategies that are prepared at national level also in other countries, you will see that in these strategies there is nowhere as detailed information about the deadlines that we want to propose in the timetable. And this is actually the most important information for the market. And this is actually a 5G implementation plan. It is this law that gives us tools to show how and when we plan to implement this new technology. I'm moving on to the other questions. There were concerns raised by Mr Pyzik about the impact the proposed solutions have on small and medium-sized entrepreneurs. I would like to point out that the Polish telecommunications market is a market which is one of the most competitive markets in Europe. We have four mobile operators, we have some of the lowest prices in Europe, but this is of course a very demanding, very expensive activity and it is not an activity for small and medium-sized enterprises. It is necessary to provide mobile services throughout the country, such business case seems most purposeful. It is therefore a tool not to support the development of small and medium-sized enterprises, but to provide services of a critical nature for all citizens today. With regard to the position of Mr Man, I would like to point out that sometimes it is not worth getting attached to what is read in the newspapers. I have even read this article and I have not found the passages that you spoke about, which means that we have probably interpreted it differently. The essence, the idea, the aim of the minister of digitalisation is to ensure the best possible mobile and stationary communications for all citizens of our country and throughout our country, to the extent possible. This is also confirmed by the current revision of the megamarket. With regard to Mrs Bubula's question on refarming, which is planned for 2020 today in line with the national spectrum action plan, I would like to point out that this national action plan will be updated within the next two weeks, as we have asked the European Commission to give us its consent to the derogation on the change of the 700 MHz band. We plan to indicate that the destination of this band will change in 2022. At the same time, we want to coordinate this process with changing the standard of transmission from DVB-T to DVB-T2. As far as costs are concerned, because the Member asked about costs, they are certainly affected by the time the multiplex operator has to prepare for migration. We have an analysis of the Communications Institute and KPMG, which says that these costs are negligible, related only to the reconfiguration. I understand that in a situation where there is little time, it changes and these costs are increasing, while it seems that due to the planned derogation these costs will be as small as originally planned. We believe that these costs should under no circumstances be borne by television broadcasters, whatever they may be. In this respect, we agree with the Member. I think I answered all the questions. Mr Bańkowski asked if we were actually extending the powers of the President of UKE by this law. Yes, this law extends the powers of the President of UKE, because that is exactly what this law aims to do. The idea is to give the UKE President the opportunity to sort out the bands that, under international agreements, must be used for other technologies. That is the essence of this law, as has already been said several times today. So much for me. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

Thank you very much to all doctors, nurses of the University Clinical Centre in Gdańsk. I know you were doing everything you could to save the President's life. Unfortunately, the injuries caused by the attacker were so extensive that they could not save his life. The people of Gdansk also showed great support, who responded very actively to the appeal of the regional center of blood donation and blood treatment, giving hundreds of liters of blood. At that time more than 521 people who gave more than 170 liters of blood came forward in Gdansk alone. This is a great gesture of solidarity, and thank you very much for that. Although the President's life could not be saved, devoted blood will probably save the health and lives of many others, including others. Gdansk. I would also like to refer the thanks to the medical emergency staff who immediately arrived at the scene and drove the President to the hospital rescue unit. At the outset, rescue operations were undertaken by the ambulance crew commissioned by the organizer to secure the event. The notification of the incident was registered in the Gdansk Medical Dispatch at an hour. 19.59. The medical dispatcher placed the team with code 1, i.e. with light and sound signals, a specialist medical rescue team consisting of four persons, including a doctor. The medical rescue team arrived at the scene at hours. 20.08. The distance from the scene to the hospital is 4 km. In the opinion of the Minister of Health, all emergency services' activities have been carried out in accordance with the procedures currently in place since the notification by the medical dispatcher to the emergency department. The information received shall be confirmed in accordance with the exit order cards and medical emergency operations cards which are at the disposal of the medical emergency team. After being admitted to the emergency unit, the victim was transferred to the O.R. immediately in accordance with the procedures in force. There, as you probably know, was followed by surgery for several hours. This procedure was attended by high-end specialists representing various kinds of specialties. They fought with great commitment for the health and life of the President. The Minister of Health, taking into account the determination with which health workers fought for their health, for the President's life, decided to grant workers who took part in this dramatic action, honour badges: deserved for health protection in Poland. The tragic death of the President raises legitimate questions about procedures for dealing with persons who are at increased risk of committing a crime after they are released. At the initiative of Prime Minister Mateusz Morawiecki, the Ministry of Health and the Ministry of Justice shall carry out a detailed analysis of the current regulations in this area and shall examine the possibility of introducing new system regulations for persons to whom the so-called regulations apply. security measures of a liberal nature which do not comply with the obligations imposed, i.e. they don't give in to therapy. The new procedure could include criminals who have been diagnosed with mental illness, personality disorder, addiction to alcohol, drugs or other substances during the execution of the sentence, as well as mental impairment. Only effective monitoring would allow monitoring of the continuation of the treatment process, the treatment initiated during the stay of the prison and the prevention of undesirable behaviour of the persons involved, as well as the control of their behaviour towards the environment. In the case of persons who fail to comply with the obligations imposed on continuation of therapy, they do not undergo treatment, the custody court could, of course, decide, depending on the degree of risk, to place them in a closed mental institution. In the current state of law, even if a mental illness manifests itself in the course of the execution of the sentence, after leaving the penitentiary, it is covered by the Law of 1994 on the Protection of Mental Health, which provides that a mentally ill person may be admitted to a psychiatric hospital without consent, first of all, when his previous conduct indicates that he or she is directly threatening his or her own life or the health of other persons because of this disease; this is Article. Article 23 (2) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1. The proposed changes to the procedure in such cases have been addressed inter alia. organized at the initiative of Prime Minister Mateusz Morawiecki meeting with representatives of clubs and parliamentary circles. In line with the findings of this meeting, a team of experts will be set up, consisting of representatives of all political groups, which will develop solutions primarily consistent with the well-being of the patient and sealing system for monitoring the behaviour of people with mental disorders. This team should be formed soon. The representatives of these formations should report their representatives as experts by the end of the week. We would like to start working immediately on solutions that will prevent future dramas such as the one we were dealing with in Gdansk. Finally, I would like to confirm the readiness and openness of the Minister of Health in relation to the substantive discussion on possible changes in this area. I hope that we can work together to develop solutions that will improve the safety of society, but will also ensure that the patient's rights are respected, and that these changes will be adopted unanimously by the Sejm. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-01-30-sejm-77-1.u382	Thank you. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Andrzej Noblet, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, will be the first to speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-01-30-sejm-77-1.u383	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I would like to present my position towards the parliamentary draft law amending the Act on the National Bank of Poland and the Act on the limitation of business activity by persons performing public functions, print no. 3160. The preparation of this project was a response to social expectations regarding the transparency of salaries of persons holding senior management positions in the National Bank of Poland. According to the current legal situation, the amount of remuneration is determined annually in the National Bank of Poland's financial plan, taking into account the level of wages in the banking sector. The rules for the remuneration of employees of the National Bank of Poland are laid down by the Management Board of the NBP and the remuneration of the President and Vice-Presidents of the NBP are determined on the basis of the rules on the remuneration of managers of state positions. The proposed bill introduces the principle of transparency as a fundamental principle of wage formation in the National Bank of Poland. The proposed rules determine the control of the authorities of the central bank of the Republic of Poland as social as well as institutional. On the other hand, granting the Management Board of the National Bank of Poland the power to further develop certain remunerations while maintaining a certain statutory maximum results from the full independence of the central bank and does not affect its autonomy to any extent. The determination of the amount of funds for remuneration remains unchanged and will be established annually in the financial plan of the National Bank of Poland. In the bill we introduce a maximum salary threshold of 0.6 times the total remuneration of the President of the National Bank of Poland. This threshold will apply to the establishment by the bank's management of remuneration for each category of posts. Art. 2 of this bill introduces the provision that the resolution of the Management Board of NBP on the principles of remuneration of NBP employees is public and is made available by the President of NBP in the Public Information Bulletin on the subject side of the National Bank of Poland. Information on the remuneration of the President, Vice President, members of the NBP's Management Board and executives listed in the Act shall be made available by the President of the National Bank of Poland in the Public Information Bulletin. Art. 3 of the bill refers to the publication by the President of the NBP in the Public Information Bulletin, within 30 days of the entry into force of this Act, of the resolution of the Management Board of the National Bank of Poland on the remuneration of NBP employees in force in the years 1995 to 2018 and on the level of remuneration received in those years by the President, Vice-Presidents, members of the Management Board and listed in the Act of the National Bank of Poland. The Act also sets out the obligation of the persons mentioned enumeratively to make a statement of their assets. The law shall enter into force on the day following that of its publication. At the same time, I make an amendment on behalf of the applicants restoring the provision of the original text of the Art Act. 66. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-01-30-sejm-77-1.u443	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Circle of Members of the Free and Solidarnia, I present my position on the report of the Committee on Public Finance concerning three parliamentary drafts for the amendment of the Act on the National Bank of Poland. All three projects respond to recent media reports on the extremely high earnings of some employees of the National Bank of Poland. I am pleased that both the government and the opposition are in agreement with the legitimacy of resolving the doubts and objections raised by the public. The National Bank of Poland is an extremely important public institution, which must inspire confidence. These projects concern the introduction of transparency of executives' earnings in the NBP. Although during the work of the NBP Vice-President's committee, the bank is currently working on the wage net which the Management Board wants to make public, we believe that the introduction of new regulations guaranteeing transparency of remuneration is appropriate. Solutions are needed, which will not only rebuild the opinion of the National Bank of Poland, but also prevent similar controversy in the future. We believe that the solutions presented are the closest to meeting social expectations is a project submitted by Members of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, which is why I think it is right to choose it as a leading project in the process. However, it is not ideal and requires amendments that will clarify it. Doubt is raised by a record limiting the level of management fees up to 60% the remuneration of the overall president of the bank. The exclusion from these regulations of vice presidents and members of the NBP Board is a step in the right direction, but the whole is still worrying me. I do not believe that such a solution limits the independence of the central bank, but I agree that it may be an obstacle to raising top qualified staff to the ranks of this institution. This argument is also in our view in favour of maintaining the provisions of the current NBP Act, which assumes that the remuneration in the bank takes into account the level of wages in the banking sector. However, I have no doubt that the National Bank of Poland is a very important part of the functioning of the state and as a public institution using public money should be transparent and inspire public confidence. Therefore, the Free and Solidarni Circle supports this bill.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-01-30-sejm-77-1.u445	Thank you. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The Committee on Administration and Home Affairs met at its meeting on 17 January 2019 to give an opinion on the amendments tabled by the Legislative Office of the Sejm, which were legislative amendments, to the government draft law amending the Law on the Council of Ministers and certain other laws, the Parliamentary printing No 3045. Ladies and gentlemen, this law, in a telegraph way, I would like to say this, concerns the granting of powers, the allocation of powers to the President of the Council of Ministers to the possibility that, in certain cases, after consulting the various heads of ministries, with ministers, he may award financial prizes for outstanding achievements, whether in the field of sport or in the field of culture or in other areas, prizes which will be financed by the state budget. Five amendments were tabled during the committee meeting. The Commission has decided to accept three of them, two of which are proposed to be rejected. These three amendments are technical, you could say, rather grammatical. Two amendments had already entered into substance, but the committee - 24 votes in favour, 4 votes against - decided to reject these amendments. The three amendments have decided to accept. Thank you very much, Marshal.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-01-30-sejm-77-1.u453	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! Innocent-sounding amendment of the Act on the Council of Ministers can, in particular, carry many traps this year, for which the club of the Polish People's Party - the Union of European Democrats, which in the government version at first reading almost unconditionally supported it, would give its consent. My club does not deny the prerogative of the President of the Council of Ministers in honoring those who deserve special distinctions for special, exceptional circumstances that directly justify the award of a special prize. We are also in favour of a statutory provision exempting special prizes from the obligation to pay personal income tax. We support the abolition of the obligation to include the amount of the special prize in the income used to determine the right to cash benefits from social assistance. Neither in the past have we stood, nor are we currently opposed to any government proposals, parliamentary initiatives that motivate innovation, which means directly motivating people for their particular, above-average, outstanding achievements both in science and in particular in the field of sport, which we are hot advocates. With regard to the project under discussion today, we are guided both by the reason of the state and reasonableness, taking into account the cases of abuse by the government of public funds during the upcoming election campaigns, when it will be easy to justify with childishness that every case was caused by an extraordinary purpose and by all reasonable reasons. To avoid this effectively, we are in favour of a very simple, logical and socially acceptable solution, so that the President of the Council of Ministers can award such a prize, but from the budget of the Chancellery of the President of the Council of Ministers. This solution will be the simplest solution to achieve the objectives set out in the proposed legislation, but which will give clarity to public spending, regardless of the duration of the granting of benefits under the draft law. All other conditions for granting special benefits in the form of prizes will remain unchanged, and the public authorities themselves, if I do not doubt that they have good goals, will remain outside the sphere of suspicion. ParlaMint-PL_2019-01-30-sejm-77-1.u455	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Of course, it is right to reward such achievements, those who have these achievements - on the one hand, these rewards allow us to appreciate the effort and undoubtedly those who achieve such outstanding results deserve it, on the other hand it is also, in a sense, the motivation that affects the fact that such people can be more. It is not surprising, then, that such regulations already exist in almost today. Well, individual ministers, individual ministries, e.g. in the field of sport, culture or science, they have the opportunity to award such awards for outstanding achievements. We have had numerous examples of whether it was for outstanding sporting achievements, as I said, or for outstanding achievements in the field of science. I must say that we are surprised to learn about this project, because it is a project that introduces a superat in this area for the President of the Council of Ministers. This is quite, I would say, a fine solution, because de facto the President of the Council of Ministers does not supervise such detailed, sketchy tasks, i.e. does not supervise sport, culture. There is a mechanism through which Law and Justice wants to create such a super-premier office. I am talking about this because this is another example of a law that conveys to the Prime Minister the competences that have been in the Ministry's hands so far, and it is good that they were in the Ministry's hands, because that is exactly what the division of tasks between individual government institutions is all about. This law in the election year, when there are elections to the European Parliament and later in the autumn, parliamentary elections, creates a mechanism by which the Prime Minister will be able to campaign. Let's face it, this election campaign of Law and Justice will be financed by the funds that will be used for prizes. How will this work? Well, the Prime Minister, like during the local government campaign, will ride around cities, ride in smaller, larger towns and give gifts. Here it will distinguish one person, there it will distinguish another person, it will of course be published in various media, on public television and it will create such an impression that here is the great host who makes a farm visit in place X, giving the hosts for outstanding achievements. This, of course, is contrary to the principles of a democratic rule of law, contrary to the principles of... [[Ha, ha, ha!]] [[That's bullshit.]] Right. Against principles, I would say, common sense, where the Prime Minister, the State, should fulfil and carry out those tasks which are appropriate to ensure the proper conditions for the functioning of citizens. On the other hand, I would say that distinctions for outstanding achievements are indeed possible and are implemented by ministers competent for these specific areas, i.e. for sport, culture, etc. To the end, I would like to say that we, the project that creates the super prime minister's office, will absolutely not support it. We believe that today's regulations are right for a modest, yet appreciative state of its citizens, those who spend their time, are determined and successful in their fields. The solutions that exist today are quite sufficient to meet the demands contained in the explanatory memorandum of this law, and the proposal which today is the subject of the High House is absolutely not necessary. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-01-30-sejm-77-1.u483	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I am pleased to present the report of the Committee on Social Policy and Family Affairs on the Government draft parenting supplemental provision law, print 3157, report of the committee - print 3172. This project is an expression of the concern of the Government of Law and Justice about the poorest citizens, seniors, including parents in families of many children. The aim of the Act is to provide means of subsistence for those who have resigned or have not taken it because of the raising of four and more children. The proposed regulation sets out the terms and conditions for granting and the rules for paying and financing parental supplementary benefit. This will be a non-contributory benefit of a special nature designed to combat the poverty of this group of persons and will be granted on request and, as a rule, will be paid by the Social Insurance Institution, the KRUS and other pension bodies paying pensions. The draft proposes that the Act enter into force on 1 March 2019 so that the benefits are paid in a new, higher amount, taking into account the valorisation of the lowest pension as from 1 March 2019. High Izbo! The project entered the Sejm on 22 January 2019 and on 3 September 2018 the draft law was submitted to the social partners for their opinion. This contribution is not covered by contributions paid during the working life, because, as I explained to the State, in the old pension scheme applicable until the end of 1999, periods of parental leave or childcare under another legal formula were not regarded as periods of contribution. Today's pension scheme based on the defined contribution principle resolves these problems differently. It solves these problems in such a way that periods of child care and cessation or suspension during this period of professional activity, periods during which an active person has so far taken care of the child, raises the child, are premium periods, are periods for which contributions to pension insurance are financed by the state budget. In the case of persons who have born and raised or raised, like certain categories of eligible persons, four children in extreme cases may be up to 16 years of age during which the state budget assumes the burden of financing pension benefits. You talk about sharing, you talk about differentiation, you talk about discrimination. I ask whether the situation in which persons born after 1948 who are covered by the new pension scheme have periods of child care taken into account in the basis of the dimension of the benefit and for which, it can be said, these periods are honoured by the State in the form of financing contributions to pension and pension insurance from the state budget, compared to the situation in which those of the generation of 70-year-olds and older people today - because we are talking about those covered by the current old pension system, the defined benefit system - are treated differently... These periods were simply not considered insurance periods as component periods. In this, I see injustice and unequal treatment of the different categories of insured persons awarded using the characteristics of the date of birth. This solution is unfair, such unequal treatment should be removed, eliminated. There has been a thread on the possibility for parents to benefit from the benefit provided for in this law. I tried to draw attention, first, to the fact that the law makes it quite possible for fathers who raise children to receive benefits to a large extent, because this possibility gives a quite clear indication that the child's mother will stop taking care of the child. In such situations, the father may apply to the President of the Social Security Office or the President of the Agricultural Insurance Fund and also apply for parental supplementary benefits. The state raises, you also raised the question of discretion earlier. During the discussion at the committee meeting, I pointed out that this discretion could be understood differently. The feature of this law is that the conditions actually obliging the President of the Social Insurance Institution or the President of the Agricultural Social Insurance Fund to issue the decision to grant the benefit are as clearly defined as possible. Yes, it will be a benefit to which the right will depend on the decision to grant the benefit. This constitutional element of the law, the constitutional element, will be the decision to grant the benefit, but as we look closer at the proposed provisions of the law, we will note that this decision of the President of the Social Security Office or KRUS will be conditional on fulfilling, as I believe, quite precisely defined conditions, such as the achievement of age - it is about 60 years in the case of a woman or 65 years in the case of a man - or the fulfilment of the conditions relating to the lack of resources to maintain the income of that person who is applying for the benefit. So, frankly, I do not share these allegations of unequal treatment or too far-reaching discretion in granting these benefits, and I also tried to draw attention to it during the committee meeting. Maybe it's worth saying a few more words about EWK's pensions, because this thread was also in the discussion. First, I paid attention to system solutions, i.e. the financing of contributions from the State budget to persons who suspend or resign from their work and raise their children during that period. It can be said that this philosophy of supporting the child's upbringing also applies to the situation in which we are dealing with the upbringing of a disabled child. Firstly, we must bear in mind that the provisions determining the length of parental leave are specially treated by parents who have taken care of the disabled child, because in this case parental leave is or can be granted twice. At that time, the period of financing the pension and pension contributions of a parent who takes care of such a child twice automatically extends from those basically 3 years of parental leave, which any parent can actually benefit from up to 6 years of age. We must also bear in mind the second solution, which is part of the philosophy of the pension system, the insurance system, namely the financing of contributions to the pension and pension insurance of carers of disabled persons in a situation where they are resigning from their employment or work, do not take and take personal care of the disabled child. In such a case, there is no time limit for the financing of contributions to pension insurance. It can be said that the legislator ensures that the financing continues until the person concerned is given the possibility to collect a pension of at least the lowest amount of pension. ParlaMint-PL_2019-01-30-sejm-77-1.u530	Thank you very much, Member. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. ParlaMint-PL_2019-01-30-sejm-77-1.u531	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! I present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice regarding the parliamentary draft law amending the Law on Higher Education and Science. The proposed amendment is practical in nature and is due to the need to clarify the rules which identify the person who, if the President's mandate expires for various reasons, will carry out his duties. The submission is general and applies to all universities. The aim of the project is to strengthen the statutory autonomy of the university by enabling it to determine on its own who, in the event of special circumstances, will perform the duties of the rector. The existing record indicates such a person, i.e. The oldest member of the university senate, however, does not give it the power to decide to perform its duties. The authors of the project propose that the powers in this area should be transferred to the level of the main document governing the structure of the university, which is the statute. The introduction of the record will facilitate the functioning of universities in situations that are de facto precedents. The person indicated in the Statute will ensure continuity and implementation of the university's policy. At the same time, the provision maintains the principle that, in the absence of statutory regulations, the duties of the rector shall be taken over by the oldest member of the Senate with at least a doctorate. The amended provision will also apply accordingly in the event of the expiry of the President's mandate as well as the suspension of the President's duties in relation to the allegations made to him. In the area of the impact of the draft law, it has a positive socio-economic impact and does not entail additional financial costs for the state budget and the budgets of local government units. The subject matter of the proposed regulation is not covered by European Union law. Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! Parliamentary Club Law and Justice appreciates this proposal and will vote in favour of it. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-01-30-sejm-77-1.u565	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Members of the applicants, I will provide a justification for the draft law amending the Act on subscription fees, print No 3123. The need for the proposed law is due to the lack of adequate, stable, predictable and long-term public funding of public media in Poland. In the long term, this shortage is made up of, among others. increasing over the years since 1993 the expansion of the circle of persons exempt from the obligation to pay subscription fees. The largest extension of these exemptions took place by virtue of the Act of 13 June 2008 amending the Act on subscription fees, which entered into force on 1 March 2010. In the course of the discussion on this law, it was pointed out that it was necessary to ensure adequate funding for the implementation of the public service broadcasting mission and, consequently, that the possible extension of the exemption from subscription fees should be accompanied by compensation for the loss of revenue resulting from such a procedure. Otherwise, the State's obligation to provide financial conditions for the performance of the public service broadcasting mission will not be fulfilled. These elements led the President of the Republic of Poland to refer the bill to the Constitutional Tribunal. In its judgment of 4 November 2009, the Court found that only part of the Act was incompatible with the Constitution, considering that the extension of the exemption from subscription fees in principle falls within the legislator's freedom to choose the form of public funding, the amount of such funding and its detailed rules. At the same time, the Constitutional Tribunal noted, and I quote: Freedom to choose the form of public financing for radiophony and television does not, at the same time, exempt the legislator from the obligation to lay down appropriate financing rules. Implementation of the public service mission by public media This is because it is not possible without the provision of adequate public funding. End of quote. Despite the fact that according to CUS 96.4% households have a television set, about half of Polish households have not registered a television or radio receiver, and among the registered ones the majority are exempt from the charge. According to the National Radio and Television Council at the end of 2017 among 6.7 million registered subscribers, households were released 3.6 million... [[We're shortening, we're shortening, we're shortening, ……]] ...of 13,6 million households in total in Poland. In other words, within 50% households that registered receivers, 53.7% benefits from exemptions, so in real terms the subscription fees are paid by around 13% society. As a result, the financing of public media in Poland has remained insufficient for years. Widening the circle of people exempt from subscription fees and facilitating the use by some of these people of dismissals has generally increased this scarcity and contributed to the systematic state of the accumulation of underfunding of Polish public radio and television. The proposed solution has already been applied once already under Article. 16a Law of 2 December 2016 on special arrangements for the implementation of the Budget Act for 2017 added by the Law of 9 November 2017 amending the Law on special arrangements established. This provision introduced a compensation of 980... [[We're done here.]] Excuse me? [[We're done, we're done.]] Are you finishing something? I'm sorry, but...

ParlaMint-PL_2019-01-30-sejm-77-1.u568	I'm asking you to calm down. [[I'm not interrupting.]]
ParlaMint-PL_2019-01-30-sejm-77-1.u569	... 980 million for lost in years... [[Let the PiS pay for...]] ... In addition, the Commission notes that the Commission's assessment of the compatibility of the aid with the internal market is based on the assessment of the compatibility of the aid with the internal market and is based on the assessment of the compatibility of the aid with the internal market and the assessment of the compatibility of the aid with the internal market. This compensation was divided by the National Radio and Television Council between public broadcasters for the implementation of their mission in 2017 and, mostly, in 2018. The compensation came from the state budget and was transferred by the Minister responsible for cultural and national heritage protection in 2017 to the separate account of the National Radio and Television Council for the collection of subscription proceeds. Subsequently, like the proceeds from subscription fees, the compensation in the relevant parts was transferred to the public broadcasters. The proposed law is intended to ensure compensation for loss resulting from the non-payment of subscription fees by persons legally exempt from that obligation in the years 2018 to 2019. The maximum amount of compensation that may be granted in this respect should be determined on the basis of the average annual number of exemptions from subscription fees in 2017 and the licence rate applicable in 2018 and 2019. This task imposes on the National Radio and Television Council, which is required to prepare a resolution in this regard. In view of the publicly available data, simulations of maximum compensation may already be carried out at this stage. These figures are as follows: the average annual amount of fees exempted from the fees on the basis of the 2017 National Council data is 3 553 244, the percentage of exempted subscribers exclusively for radio, the data of the National Council, the condition as at 31 December 2017, is 1,68, the licence fee RTV for 2019 is PLN 22,70, for radio - PLN 7. So the loss of revenue is PLN 956 653 344. The compensation should not exceed that amount. Therefore, the maximum compensation for public broadcasting units for the years 2018 to 2019 would be PLN 1 913 306 888. However, it is proposed that this compensation should amount to PLN 1260 million between 2018 and 2019. The compensation mechanism proposed in the draft law is based on the issue of securities. It is in fact similar to the mechanism used for compensation for the years 2010 and 2017. Both then and now, the National Radio and Television Council will have an impact on how funding will be divided between individual public radio and television units. On the basis of a resolution of the National Council setting out the amount of compensation and the distribution of treasury securities between the companies, the Minister of Culture and National Heritage shall request the Minister of Finance to transfer the Treasury securities of the nominal value indicated in that resolution of the National Council. The Minister of Finance shall issue treasury bonds on this basis and shall transmit them in accordance with the decision set out in the resolution of the national council designated to public radio and television units. Members of the applicant request the adoption of the bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
