ParlaMint-PL_2019-01-30-sejm-77-1.u571	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! I present the opinion of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice on the draft law amending the Act on subscription fees, contained in print No 3123. To begin with, the bill presented should not surprise anyone or surprise anyone. This is in a sense a repetition of the principle of compensation of Polish Television, Polish Radio, already adopted more than a year ago and, which I particularly want to stress, 17 regional companies of public radio losses, which are incurred annually by exempting millions of our citizens from paying a radio and television subscription. More than 3.5 million subscribers are exempt from this public tribute, according to the calculations just presented by the Member, while nearly 14 million households should pay such a subscription. The exemptions have been introduced by law, so it is the legislator's duty to indicate another source of funding for the public service mission. This is what happens in every civilized country and this is what happens in Poland in many other areas of life. This has also been the case for radio and television for 1.5 years. This is the principle we adopted when we passed the previous Compensation Act. However, we now have one more reason to adopt a law that gives the national media the public funds they deserve. Last year, the High Sejm decided to adopt the bill expected by all political forces, a law amending the law on radio and television, defining and clarifying the so-called. a public media duty card. This law clarifies the concept of public service and the principle of ordering certain services and programmes by the State in the form of a duty card, namely the precise calculation of the species and content expected in the public media for the sake of the common good. And of these content and of this kind of procurement, public media are to be settled in accordance with the law. The Charter of Obligations adopted by the National Radio and Television Council defines in a 5-year perspective what will be produced and presented to audiences and listeners in terms of the implementation of emissions. This Act therefore establishes only part of the necessary financing rules, which will enable the Polish Television and Polish Radio to draw up a list of the costs of such planned programmes and programmes. As you know, the duty card is supposed to have a five-year perspective. This Act compensates for a 2-year loss in subscription receipts. If we want the duty card to be in line with the objectives set by the High Sejm in the Act adopted less than a year ago, then we are only past 2/5 ways in terms of this funding. The public mission covers a wide range of content, including expensive, non-advertising content. It is necessary to finance educational programmes, programmes for children, animated films, documentaries and historical series. It is necessary to finance programmes in the field of high culture, raising the level of knowledge and sensitivity in the field of music, theatre, literature, film, art, architecture. There is a need for expenditure on natural films, for great film productions about the history of Poland, for biographical films about outstanding figures of our art and national heroes. No less important is civic education raising awareness of the principles of state action, teaching active participation in public life, introducing a culture of discussion and argumentation. There are many demands about the need for legal and economic education of millions of compatriots. ParlaMint-PL_2019-01-30-sejm-77-1.u646	Thank you very much. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mrs Lidia Burzyńska, Law and Justice will be the first to speak. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2019-01-30-sejm-77-1.u647	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the law and justice club, I present my position on the draft law amending the electoral code law, print no. 3163. The bill contains amendments to the Act, which the Sejm passed on 11 January 2018, which concern inter alia. adapting a number of regulations to all types of choices. Citizens will be able to make applications for the personal compositions of the regional committees, not as they have been so far, that only electoral committees could do so. It is also important to deprive the passive electoral right of persons convicted of crimes prosecuted by public prosecutions or criminal and treasure crimes, of any penalty, as well as the setting up of regional electoral committees in various formations, depending on the size of the voting circuit, of allowing a European Union citizen who is not a Polish citizen to vote in elections to the European Parliament on the basis of a certificate of the right to vote at the place of residence on the day of the election, of course issued by the consul who drew up the electoral census. It should be noted that the project does not entail additional financial costs for the budgets of local government units, on the contrary, a revision of the rules on the setting up of two regional committees only in the elections to local government units carried out in connection with the end of the term of office of the Councils will contribute to saving in the budget of the State the costs allocated to the holding of elections. There is also no doubt that its introduction will have a positive social impact, e.g. to increase the availability of certain activities or functions. In view of the above, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will vote in favour of the parliamentary bill amending the Code of Elections. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2019-01-30-sejm-77-1.u648	Thank you, Member.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-01-30-sejm-77-1.u657	Dear Marshal! Mr. Messengers! Gentlemen! On behalf of the Poselski Club Modern I submit my position to the draft law amending the electoral code law. The proposed law is undoubtedly part of the category of system laws, and therefore particular care should be taken in relation to such laws concerning the procedure for their adoption. In this case, however, we are once again dealing with a law which is not subject to any public consultation, which is, moreover, pushed by the High House on a super-express date. In fact, one day you want to make a lot of changes to this law. This clearly indicates that at this rate we will surely return to the electoral code very quickly, because this bill will be another legal bubble. This is indicated by the work itself on this law and issues related to some of the good rules which correct the manifest errors made in the previous year, i.e. issues relating to two committees. But here, too, the applicants did not avoid mistakes because they only changed one rule when it is necessary to amend dozens of provisions in the electoral code law. All this indicates that we are dealing with legislative sloppyness, which will result in the need for a quick revision of this. Another aspect concerns the constitutionality of the law. Firstly, in the settled case-law of the Constitutional Tribunal, there is a dominant view that changes in the electoral code, major changes, cannot be made within 6 months before the date of the announcement of the elections. This makes the entire bill unconstitutional, because a number of regulations are essential provisions that affect the amendment of the electoral code. The second aspect of non-constitutionality is the issue of introducing requirements, different requirements, in terms of defining passive electoral rights vis-à-vis voivodships, mayors and city presidents, and different requirements vis-à-vis Members, Senators and President of the Republic. These different requirements indicate that there is a breach of the principle of equal access to public service. This is not only about the fact that people who will be sentenced to imprisonment for an intentional crime prosecuted by public prosecution will not be able to run for office, but this provision will also apply to the mayors, mayors, city presidents who will commit this kind of offence during their term of office. If these persons commit such an offence, a procedure will be initiated to remove them from office. However, we do not have this type of procedure in the case of Members and Senators. Let us take ourselves seriously and treat all those who perform public functions equally, not selectively. For these reasons, due to obvious constitutional defects, although the law contains several provisions that actually simplify electoral law and return to the already proven mechanisms, the Polish Club The modern proposal to reject the draft law in its entirety at second reading. [[Applause]]
